                "data": {
                  "data": {
                    "title": "Add threshold-based Traffic Light dashboard widget",
                    "description": "## Goal\nImplement a new first-class **Traffic Light** dashboard widget that lets a user select a single numeric item and configure thresholds that deterministically map the item’s latest value to exactly one visual state: **GREEN**, **YELLOW**, or **RED**.\n\nThe widget must fit the existing dashboard widget architecture and lifecycle, including add/edit/save/reopen/refresh flows, reuse current item access and permission rules, and render a clear fallback state for missing, unsupported, inaccessible, or no-data cases.\n\n**Threshold semantics must be explicit in implementation and tests.** This ticket requires choosing and documenting one supported threshold model and one boundary rule for equality (`= threshold`), then applying that behavior consistently in validation, evaluation, rendering, and automated tests.\n\n## Scope\nIn scope:\n- Add a new dashboard widget type under the existing widget framework.\n- Register the widget so it is available in the widget picker and behaves like other first-class widgets.\n- Provide configuration for:\n  - exactly one monitored item\n  - threshold inputs required by the chosen three-state model\n- Validate required fields, numeric threshold input, supported item type, and valid threshold ordering.\n- Retrieve the latest value for the selected item using existing dashboard/item access patterns.\n- Evaluate the latest numeric value into exactly one state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- Return and render deterministic fallback states/messages for:\n  - missing item\n  - inaccessible item / permission denied\n  - unsupported item type\n  - no latest value\n- Integrate with normal dashboard refresh/update behavior so the state updates when the latest item value changes.\n- Preserve configuration across dashboard add/save/reopen/edit flows.\n- Add localization strings for widget name, config fields, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- Add automated coverage for validation, threshold evaluation, persistence, rendering, refresh behavior, and permissions.\n\nOut of scope:\n- Multi-item aggregation or rollups\n- Non-numeric thresholding\n- Arbitrary color schemes or more than three states\n- Advanced alerting logic beyond visual state display\n- New item retrieval/polling infrastructure outside existing widget/data access conventions\n\nSystem boundaries / changed surfaces:\n- Dashboard widget registration/catalog\n- Widget config form and persistence payload\n- Server-side widget view/action for render data\n- Threshold evaluation logic (single source of truth)\n- Client-side widget rendering/template/CSS\n- Localization resources\n- Automated tests across backend and UI/widget layers\n\n## System Changes\n- Add a new widget package under the existing UI widget architecture, following existing conventions for manifest, JS widget class, server-side view action, template, and styling.\n- Register the **Traffic Light** widget in the dashboard widget catalog so users can add, configure, save, reopen, and render it through standard flows.\n- Define widget configuration fields for:\n  - one selected item\n  - threshold values required by the chosen semantics\n- Implement server-side validation for:\n  - required item selection\n  - numeric threshold input\n  - supported numeric item type\n  - valid threshold ordering\n- Implement a single threshold evaluation path that maps one numeric value to one state only, plus fallback states such as `NO_DATA`, `UNSUPPORTED`, or `INACCESSIBLE`.\n- Reuse the existing latest-value retrieval flow used by dashboard widgets instead of introducing a new history or polling path.\n- Ensure refresh requests re-evaluate the latest value and current state without requiring widget reconfiguration.\n- Apply existing authorization and item visibility rules to both selection and render-time retrieval.\n- Render exactly three indicator lights, with only one active indicator for valid states and a deterministic fallback presentation for non-renderable states.\n- Display item name and formatted latest value only if this aligns with existing widget UX conventions and can reuse current formatting helpers.\n- Add localized widget metadata, labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- A user can add a **Traffic Light** widget from the dashboard widget picker.\n- The widget configuration supports selecting exactly one item and entering threshold values required by the implemented threshold model.\n- Saving and reopening the widget preserves the selected item and threshold configuration.\n- The selected item must be validated as a supported numeric item type; unsupported non-numeric types are rejected or rendered as unsupported according to existing widget conventions.\n- Threshold ordering and equality behavior are explicitly defined in code and covered by automated tests.\n- For a supported numeric item with a latest value, the widget evaluates the value into exactly one active state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- The rendered widget never shows multiple active lights at once.\n- If the selected item is missing, inaccessible, unsupported, or has no latest value, the widget shows a deterministic fallback state/message instead of an incorrect color.\n- The widget updates through the normal dashboard refresh lifecycle when the selected item’s latest value changes.\n- Users cannot obtain item data through this widget unless they are authorized to read that item.\n- Localized labels/messages exist for the widget title, config fields, validation errors, and fallback states.\n- Automated tests covering validation, evaluation, persistence, rendering, refresh behavior, and permissions pass.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n### Backend\n- [ ] Create a new widget module directory under the existing `ui/widgets` structure following established widget conventions.\n- [ ] Add widget metadata/manifest including widget id, name, namespace, action mapping/URL, defaults, JS class reference, and any required framework metadata.\n- [ ] Implement the server-side widget view/action that loads persisted config, resolves the selected item, applies permission checks, fetches the latest value, evaluates thresholds, and returns render data.\n- [ ] Add or extend widget form handling so the selected item id and thresholds are persisted in the widget configuration payload.\n- [ ] Validate required item selection, numeric threshold values, supported item value types, and valid threshold ordering.\n- [ ] Define one explicit threshold model and equality behavior for boundary values, and implement it as the single source of truth used during render-time evaluation.\n- [ ] Return deterministic fallback states for missing item, inaccessible item, unsupported item type, and no latest value.\n- [ ] Reuse existing item formatting helpers if the render payload includes item label and/or formatted value.\n- [ ] Ensure refresh-time requests re-evaluate state from the latest value using the standard dashboard lifecycle.\n- [ ] Add localization keys for backend-generated labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n\n### Frontend\n- [ ] Add a client-side widget class compatible with the existing dashboard widget lifecycle and wired from the widget manifest.\n- [ ] Implement widget rendering with exactly three indicator lights and only one active light for valid states.\n- [ ] Implement fallback UI for non-renderable states such as `NO_DATA`, `UNSUPPORTED`, or `INACCESSIBLE`, aligned with existing widget error/cover patterns.\n- [ ] Add widget configuration UI using existing item selector controls and numeric threshold field components where possible.\n- [ ] Add client-side validation for obvious required-field, numeric-input, and threshold-order issues while keeping server-side validation authoritative.\n- [ ] Ensure add/edit/save/reopen flows load and display persisted configuration correctly.\n- [ ] Ensure refresh/update behavior does not leave stale state or duplicate active indicators.\n- [ ] Add styles/assets that fit current dashboard theming and accessibility conventions.\n- [ ] Add localization strings for widget title, field labels, helper text, and fallback/state messages.\n\n### Testing\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for validation rules covering missing item, missing thresholds, non-numeric input, invalid ordering, duplicate/equal threshold scenarios if unsupported, and supported versus unsupported item types.\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for threshold evaluation covering values below, at, between, and above thresholds, with explicit assertions for equality behavior at every threshold boundary.\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for fallback handling for missing item id, inaccessible item, unsupported item type, and no latest value.\n- [ ] Add integration tests for widget save/load persistence across add, save, reopen, and edit flows.\n- [ ] Add integration tests for data retrieval and dashboard refresh so the active state changes when the underlying latest value changes.\n- [ ] Add permission-focused tests to verify users without item read access cannot retrieve item data through the widget.\n- [ ] Add frontend/rendering tests to verify only one light is active for valid states and fallback UI is shown for non-renderable states.\n- [ ] Run relevant project test suites for the touched backend and UI/widget areas using repository-standard helpers.\n- [ ] Manual verification: add the widget to a real dashboard, configure a seeded numeric item, and confirm visual rendering of GREEN, YELLOW, and RED states.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify threshold boundary behavior by setting exact threshold values and confirming the active light matches the documented equality semantics.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify save/reopen/edit flows preserve configuration and recalculate state correctly after threshold changes.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify refresh behavior by changing the latest item value and confirming the widget updates through the normal dashboard refresh lifecycle.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify fallback behavior for unsupported/no-data/inaccessible scenarios and confirm the widget does not show an incorrect active color.\n\n## Verification\nAutomated / tooling-based verification:\n- Run targeted unit and integration tests for the new widget module, threshold evaluator, and widget persistence/render paths.\n- Run relevant UI/widget test suites and backend test suites using repository-standard helpers (for example, `tfh-test` and targeted test invocations where available).\n- If needed, build and start the local stack using repository-standard helpers (for example, `tfh-build`, `tfh-start`, or equivalent) to validate end-to-end widget behavior.\n- Inspect logs for widget action/render failures during refresh or permission checks.\n\nManual verification:\n- Add the widget from the dashboard widget picker and confirm it appears and saves successfully.\n- Configure a supported numeric item and verify each state with representative values below, at, between, and above thresholds.\n- Verify exact threshold-edge behavior using boundary values equal to configured thresholds.\n- Reopen the widget configuration after save and confirm the selected item and thresholds persist.\n- Verify the widget updates after dashboard refresh when the latest value changes.\n- Verify behavior with a user lacking read access to the selected item.\n- Verify fallback rendering for missing item, unsupported type, and no-data cases.\n\n## Documentation Impact\n- Add or update widget catalog/help documentation for the new **Traffic Light** widget.\n- Document the chosen threshold semantics clearly, including required ordering and how equality at boundaries is resolved.\n- Add/update localization resources and translation source entries for widget labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- If the repository includes widget architecture or developer notes, add a short note describing how this widget is registered and how it reuses the standard latest-value and dashboard refresh lifecycle.\n\n## Suggested Diagram\n```puml\n@startuml\ntitle Traffic Light widget data flow\n\nactor User\nparticipant \"Widget config form\\n(item + yellow/red thresholds)\" as Form\nparticipant \"Server-side form validation\\n+ widget save handler\" as Save\ncollections \"Persisted widget config\" as Config\nparticipant \"Dashboard refresh lifecycle\\n/widget action\" as View\nparticipant \"Item access + permission\\n+ latest value retrieval\" as ItemFlow\nparticipant \"Threshold evaluator\\n(single source of truth)\" as Eval\nparticipant \"Traffic Light template/JS\\nrender state\" as Render\n\nUser -> Form : Select item\\nEnter thresholds\nForm -> Save : Submit config\nSave -> Save : Validate required item\\nValidate numeric inputs\\nValidate supported numeric type\\nValidate threshold ordering\nSave -> Config : Persist itemid + thresholds\n\nloop Open widget / dashboard refresh\n  View -> Config : Load widget config\n  View -> ItemFlow : Resolve item + check visibility\\nFetch latest value\n\n  alt Item missing or denied\n    ItemFlow --> View : INACCESSIBLE\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else Unsupported item type\n    ItemFlow --> View : UNSUPPORTED\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else No latest value\n    ItemFlow --> View : NO_DATA\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else Numeric latest value\n    ItemFlow --> View : value\n    View -> Eval : Evaluate(value, thresholds)\n    Eval --> View : GREEN or YELLOW or RED\n    View -> Render : Render exactly one active light\\n+ optional formatted item/value\n  end\nend\n\n@enduml\n```",

= $data['traffic_light'];\n+\n+$body = (new CDiv())\n+\t->addClass('traffic-light-widget')\n+\t->addClass('traffic-light-widget-state-'.strtolower($traffic_light['state']));\n+\n+$indicators = (new CDiv())->addClass('traffic-light-widget-indicators');\n+\n+foreach ([\n+\tCTrafficLightWidgetHelper::STATE_GREEN => 'green',\n+\tCTrafficLightWidgetHelper::STATE_YELLOW => 'yellow',\n+\tCTrafficLightWidgetHelper::STATE_RED => 'red'\n+] as $state => $color) {\n+\t$indicator = (new CDiv())\n+\t\t->addClass('traffic-light-widget-indicator')\n+\t\t->addClass('traffic-light-widget-indicator-'.$color);\n+\n+\tif ($traffic_light['state'] === $state && !$traffic_light['is_fallback']) {\n+\t\t$indicator->addClass('traffic-light-widget-indicator-active');\n+\t}\n+\n+\t$indicators->addItem($indicator);\n+}\n+\n+$body->addItem($indicators);\n+\n+$details = (new CDiv())->addClass('traffic-light-widget-details');\n+\n+if ($traffic_light['label'] !== '') {\n+\t$details->addItem(\n+\t\t(new CDiv($traffic_light['label']))->addClass('traffic-light-widget-label')\n+\t);\n+}\n+\n+if ($traffic_light['value'] !== '') {\n+\t$details->addItem(\n+\t\t(new CDiv($traffic_light['value']))->addClass('traffic-light-widget-value')\n+\t);\n+}\n+\n+$details->addItem(\n+\t(new CDiv($traffic_light['is_fallback'] ? $traffic_light['message'] : $traffic_light['state']))\n+\t\t->addClass('traffic-light-widget-message')\n+);\n+\n+$body->addItem($details);\n+\n+if (!$traffic_light['is_fallback'] && $traffic_light['url'] !== '') {\n+\t$body = new CLink($body, $traffic_light['url']);\n+}\n+\n+(new CWidgetView($data))\n+\t->addItem($body)\n+\t->show();\n\ntokens used\n370,363\nImplemented the traffic light dashboard widget in [manifest.json](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/widgets/trafficlight/manifest.json), [WidgetForm.php](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/widgets/trafficlight/includes/WidgetForm.php), [WidgetView.php](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/widgets/trafficlight/actions/WidgetView.php), [widget.view.php](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/widgets/trafficlight/views/widget.view.php), and [widget.css](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/widgets/trafficlight/assets/css/widget.css). The threshold model is explicit and consistent: `< yellow = GREEN`, `>= yellow and < red = YELLOW`, `>= red = RED`. Fallback rendering is stable for missing/inaccessible items, unsupported item types, and no-data cases.\n\nAdded the shared evaluator in [CTrafficLightWidgetHelper.php](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/include/classes/helpers/CTrafficLightWidgetHelper.php), unit coverage in [CTrafficLightWidgetHelperTest.php](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/tests/unit/include/classes/helpers/CTrafficLightWidgetHelperTest.php), and Selenium coverage in [testDashboardTrafficLightWidget.php](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/tests/selenium/dashboardWidgets/testDashboardTrafficLightWidget.php). I also updated the backlog notebook and step checklist in [e1d89ba1-3016-4d4e-af14-27df5c935e46.md](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/.ploqa/backlog/notebooks/e1d89ba1-3016-4d4e-af14-27df5c935e46.md) and [step.md](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/.ploqa/backlog/runs/e1d89ba1-3016-4d4e-af14-27df5c935e46/2026-04-18T15-05-36-361822-00-00/implement/implement/step.md).\n\nVerification completed here: `php -l` on the new PHP files, manifest JSON validation, and a direct PHP smoke check of threshold parsing/evaluation. Repo-managed suites are still blocked in this worktree because `phpunit` is not installed, `vendor/bin/phpunit` is absent, and `.ploqa/commands/commands.json` / `tfh-*` helpers are not present. The step checklist reflects that blocker, so the workflow step is not marked complete.\n\n`checklist_update.additions`: implement widget module; add targeted tests; run local verification; run repo-managed suites when tooling is available.  \n`checklist_update.updates`: first three items marked `[x]`; repo-managed suite execution left `[ ]` due missing tooling.\n[launcher] Codex exec exit code: 0\n\n\nSTDERR:\nWarning: Permanently added '[host.docker.internal]:2226' (ED25519) to the list of known hosts.\n\n\n## 5. sync workspace back to host\n$ ssh -i /workspace/.qemu/id_ed25519 -p 2226 -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=5 -o ConnectionAttempts=1 dev@host.docker.internal -- 'cd /home/dev/workspaces/b-3e66cb39-9875b178c858 && tar -cf - --exclude dbdata --exclude database_storage --exclude redis_data --exclude node_modules --exclude .pnpm-store --exclude .expo --exclude .local --exclude .cache --exclude .npm --exclude dist --exclude build --exclude .ploqa/codex-state --exclude '\"'\"'*/.ploqa/codex-state'\"'\"' --exclude .ploqa/tmp/codex --exclude '\"'\"'*/.ploqa/tmp/codex'\"'\"' --exclude .local --exclude .cache --exclude .npm --exclude .git --exclude .ploqa --exclude .launcher --exclude .mypy_cache --exclude '\"'\"'*/.mypy_cache'\"'\"' --exclude __pycache__ --exclude '\"'\"'*/__pycache__'\"'\"' --exclude .pytest_cache --exclude '\"'\"'*/.pytest_cache'\"'\"' --exclude .ruff_cache --exclude '\"'\"'*/.ruff_cache'\"'\"' --exclude .tox --exclude '\"'\"'*/.tox'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'.codex_runner_*'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/appendonlydir'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/pgdata'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/postgres_data'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/mysql_data'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/mongo_data'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/storage'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/uploads'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/tmp'\"'\"' .' '|' tar --overwrite -xf - -C /workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b\nRETURN CODE: 0\n\nSTDOUT:\n\n\nSTDERR:\nWarning: Permanently added '[host.docker.internal]:2226' (ED25519) to the list of known hosts.\n",
                "data": {
                  "data": {
                    "title": "Add threshold-based Traffic Light dashboard widget",
                    "description": "## Goal\nImplement a new first-class **Traffic Light** dashboard widget that lets a user select a single numeric item and configure thresholds that deterministically map the item’s latest value to exactly one visual state: **GREEN**, **YELLOW**, or **RED**.\n\nThe widget must fit the existing dashboard widget architecture and lifecycle, including add/edit/save/reopen/refresh flows, reuse current item access and permission rules, and render a clear fallback state for missing, unsupported, inaccessible, or no-data cases.\n\n**Threshold semantics must be explicit in implementation and tests.** This ticket requires choosing and documenting one supported threshold model and one boundary rule for equality (`= threshold`), then applying that behavior consistently in validation, evaluation, rendering, and automated tests.\n\n## Scope\nIn scope:\n- Add a new dashboard widget type under the existing widget framework.\n- Register the widget so it is available in the widget picker and behaves like other first-class widgets.\n- Provide configuration for:\n  - exactly one monitored item\n  - threshold inputs required by the chosen three-state model\n- Validate required fields, numeric threshold input, supported item type, and valid threshold ordering.\n- Retrieve the latest value for the selected item using existing dashboard/item access patterns.\n- Evaluate the latest numeric value into exactly one state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- Return and render deterministic fallback states/messages for:\n  - missing item\n  - inaccessible item / permission denied\n  - unsupported item type\n  - no latest value\n- Integrate with normal dashboard refresh/update behavior so the state updates when the latest item value changes.\n- Preserve configuration across dashboard add/save/reopen/edit flows.\n- Add localization strings for widget name, config fields, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- Add automated coverage for validation, threshold evaluation, persistence, rendering, refresh behavior, and permissions.\n\nOut of scope:\n- Multi-item aggregation or rollups\n- Non-numeric thresholding\n- Arbitrary color schemes or more than three states\n- Advanced alerting logic beyond visual state display\n- New item retrieval/polling infrastructure outside existing widget/data access conventions\n\nSystem boundaries / changed surfaces:\n- Dashboard widget registration/catalog\n- Widget config form and persistence payload\n- Server-side widget view/action for render data\n- Threshold evaluation logic (single source of truth)\n- Client-side widget rendering/template/CSS\n- Localization resources\n- Automated tests across backend and UI/widget layers\n\n## System Changes\n- Add a new widget package under the existing UI widget architecture, following existing conventions for manifest, JS widget class, server-side view action, template, and styling.\n- Register the **Traffic Light** widget in the dashboard widget catalog so users can add, configure, save, reopen, and render it through standard flows.\n- Define widget configuration fields for:\n  - one selected item\n  - threshold values required by the chosen semantics\n- Implement server-side validation for:\n  - required item selection\n  - numeric threshold input\n  - supported numeric item type\n  - valid threshold ordering\n- Implement a single threshold evaluation path that maps one numeric value to one state only, plus fallback states such as `NO_DATA`, `UNSUPPORTED`, or `INACCESSIBLE`.\n- Reuse the existing latest-value retrieval flow used by dashboard widgets instead of introducing a new history or polling path.\n- Ensure refresh requests re-evaluate the latest value and current state without requiring widget reconfiguration.\n- Apply existing authorization and item visibility rules to both selection and render-time retrieval.\n- Render exactly three indicator lights, with only one active indicator for valid states and a deterministic fallback presentation for non-renderable states.\n- Display item name and formatted latest value only if this aligns with existing widget UX conventions and can reuse current formatting helpers.\n- Add localized widget metadata, labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- A user can add a **Traffic Light** widget from the dashboard widget picker.\n- The widget configuration supports selecting exactly one item and entering threshold values required by the implemented threshold model.\n- Saving and reopening the widget preserves the selected item and threshold configuration.\n- The selected item must be validated as a supported numeric item type; unsupported non-numeric types are rejected or rendered as unsupported according to existing widget conventions.\n- Threshold ordering and equality behavior are explicitly defined in code and covered by automated tests.\n- For a supported numeric item with a latest value, the widget evaluates the value into exactly one active state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- The rendered widget never shows multiple active lights at once.\n- If the selected item is missing, inaccessible, unsupported, or has no latest value, the widget shows a deterministic fallback state/message instead of an incorrect color.\n- The widget updates through the normal dashboard refresh lifecycle when the selected item’s latest value changes.\n- Users cannot obtain item data through this widget unless they are authorized to read that item.\n- Localized labels/messages exist for the widget title, config fields, validation errors, and fallback states.\n- Automated tests covering validation, evaluation, persistence, rendering, refresh behavior, and permissions pass.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n### Backend\n- [ ] Create a new widget module directory under the existing `ui/widgets` structure following established widget conventions.\n- [ ] Add widget metadata/manifest including widget id, name, namespace, action mapping/URL, defaults, JS class reference, and any required framework metadata.\n- [ ] Implement the server-side widget view/action that loads persisted config, resolves the selected item, applies permission checks, fetches the latest value, evaluates thresholds, and returns render data.\n- [ ] Add or extend widget form handling so the selected item id and thresholds are persisted in the widget configuration payload.\n- [ ] Validate required item selection, numeric threshold values, supported item value types, and valid threshold ordering.\n- [ ] Define one explicit threshold model and equality behavior for boundary values, and implement it as the single source of truth used during render-time evaluation.\n- [ ] Return deterministic fallback states for missing item, inaccessible item, unsupported item type, and no latest value.\n- [ ] Reuse existing item formatting helpers if the render payload includes item label and/or formatted value.\n- [ ] Ensure refresh-time requests re-evaluate state from the latest value using the standard dashboard lifecycle.\n- [ ] Add localization keys for backend-generated labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n\n### Frontend\n- [ ] Add a client-side widget class compatible with the existing dashboard widget lifecycle and wired from the widget manifest.\n- [ ] Implement widget rendering with exactly three indicator lights and only one active light for valid states.\n- [ ] Implement fallback UI for non-renderable states such as `NO_DATA`, `UNSUPPORTED`, or `INACCESSIBLE`, aligned with existing widget error/cover patterns.\n- [ ] Add widget configuration UI using existing item selector controls and numeric threshold field components where possible.\n- [ ] Add client-side validation for obvious required-field, numeric-input, and threshold-order issues while keeping server-side validation authoritative.\n- [ ] Ensure add/edit/save/reopen flows load and display persisted configuration correctly.\n- [ ] Ensure refresh/update behavior does not leave stale state or duplicate active indicators.\n- [ ] Add styles/assets that fit current dashboard theming and accessibility conventions.\n- [ ] Add localization strings for widget title, field labels, helper text, and fallback/state messages.\n\n### Testing\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for validation rules covering missing item, missing thresholds, non-numeric input, invalid ordering, duplicate/equal threshold scenarios if unsupported, and supported versus unsupported item types.\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for threshold evaluation covering values below, at, between, and above thresholds, with explicit assertions for equality behavior at every threshold boundary.\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for fallback handling for missing item id, inaccessible item, unsupported item type, and no latest value.\n- [ ] Add integration tests for widget save/load persistence across add, save, reopen, and edit flows.\n- [ ] Add integration tests for data retrieval and dashboard refresh so the active state changes when the underlying latest value changes.\n- [ ] Add permission-focused tests to verify users without item read access cannot retrieve item data through the widget.\n- [ ] Add frontend/rendering tests to verify only one light is active for valid states and fallback UI is shown for non-renderable states.\n- [ ] Run relevant project test suites for the touched backend and UI/widget areas using repository-standard helpers.\n- [ ] Manual verification: add the widget to a real dashboard, configure a seeded numeric item, and confirm visual rendering of GREEN, YELLOW, and RED states.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify threshold boundary behavior by setting exact threshold values and confirming the active light matches the documented equality semantics.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify save/reopen/edit flows preserve configuration and recalculate state correctly after threshold changes.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify refresh behavior by changing the latest item value and confirming the widget updates through the normal dashboard refresh lifecycle.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify fallback behavior for unsupported/no-data/inaccessible scenarios and confirm the widget does not show an incorrect active color.\n\n## Verification\nAutomated / tooling-based verification:\n- Run targeted unit and integration tests for the new widget module, threshold evaluator, and widget persistence/render paths.\n- Run relevant UI/widget test suites and backend test suites using repository-standard helpers (for example, `tfh-test` and targeted test invocations where available).\n- If needed, build and start the local stack using repository-standard helpers (for example, `tfh-build`, `tfh-start`, or equivalent) to validate end-to-end widget behavior.\n- Inspect logs for widget action/render failures during refresh or permission checks.\n\nManual verification:\n- Add the widget from the dashboard widget picker and confirm it appears and saves successfully.\n- Configure a supported numeric item and verify each state with representative values below, at, between, and above thresholds.\n- Verify exact threshold-edge behavior using boundary values equal to configured thresholds.\n- Reopen the widget configuration after save and confirm the selected item and thresholds persist.\n- Verify the widget updates after dashboard refresh when the latest value changes.\n- Verify behavior with a user lacking read access to the selected item.\n- Verify fallback rendering for missing item, unsupported type, and no-data cases.\n\n## Documentation Impact\n- Add or update widget catalog/help documentation for the new **Traffic Light** widget.\n- Document the chosen threshold semantics clearly, including required ordering and how equality at boundaries is resolved.\n- Add/update localization resources and translation source entries for widget labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- If the repository includes widget architecture or developer notes, add a short note describing how this widget is registered and how it reuses the standard latest-value and dashboard refresh lifecycle.\n\n## Suggested Diagram\n```puml\n@startuml\ntitle Traffic Light widget data flow\n\nactor User\nparticipant \"Widget config form\\n(item + yellow/red thresholds)\" as Form\nparticipant \"Server-side form validation\\n+ widget save handler\" as Save\ncollections \"Persisted widget config\" as Config\nparticipant \"Dashboard refresh lifecycle\\n/widget action\" as View\nparticipant \"Item access + permission\\n+ latest value retrieval\" as ItemFlow\nparticipant \"Threshold evaluator\\n(single source of truth)\" as Eval\nparticipant \"Traffic Light template/JS\\nrender state\" as Render\n\nUser -> Form : Select item\\nEnter thresholds\nForm -> Save : Submit config\nSave -> Save : Validate required item\\nValidate numeric inputs\\nValidate supported numeric type\\nValidate threshold ordering\nSave -> Config : Persist itemid + thresholds\n\nloop Open widget / dashboard refresh\n  View -> Config : Load widget config\n  View -> ItemFlow : Resolve item + check visibility\\nFetch latest value\n\n  alt Item missing or denied\n    ItemFlow --> View : INACCESSIBLE\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else Unsupported item type\n    ItemFlow --> View : UNSUPPORTED\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else No latest value\n    ItemFlow --> View : NO_DATA\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else Numeric latest value\n    ItemFlow --> View : value\n    View -> Eval : Evaluate(value, thresholds)\n    Eval --> View : GREEN or YELLOW or RED\n    View -> Render : Render exactly one active light\\n+ optional formatted item/value\n  end\nend\n\n@enduml\n```",

confirming the widget updates through the normal dashboard refresh lifecycle.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify fallback behavior for unsupported/no-data/inaccessible scenarios and confirm the widget does not show an incorrect active color.\n\n## Verification\nAutomated / tooling-based verification:\n- Run targeted unit and integration tests for the new widget module, threshold evaluator, and widget persistence/render paths.\n- Run relevant UI/widget test suites and backend test suites using repository-standard helpers (for example, `tfh-test` and targeted test invocations where available).\n- If needed, build and start the local stack using repository-standard helpers (for example, `tfh-build`, `tfh-start`, or equivalent) to validate end-to-end widget behavior.\n- Inspect logs for widget action/render failures during refresh or permission checks.\n\nManual verification:\n- Add the widget from the dashboard widget picker and confirm it appears and saves successfully.\n- Configure a supported numeric item and verify each state with representative values below, at, between, and above thresholds.\n- Verify exact threshold-edge behavior using boundary values equal to configured thresholds.\n- Reopen the widget configuration after save and confirm the selected item and thresholds persist.\n- Verify the widget updates after dashboard refresh when the latest value changes.\n- Verify behavior with a user lacking read access to the selected item.\n- Verify fallback rendering for missing item, unsupported type, and no-data cases.\n\n## Documentation Impact\n- Add or update widget catalog/help documentation for the new **Traffic Light** widget.\n- Document the chosen threshold semantics clearly, including required ordering and how equality at boundaries is resolved.\n- Add/update localization resources and translation source entries for widget labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- If the repository includes widget architecture or developer notes, add a short note describing how this widget is registered and how it reuses the standard latest-value and dashboard refresh lifecycle.\n\n## Suggested Diagram\n```puml\n@startuml\ntitle Traffic Light widget data flow\n\nactor User\nparticipant \"Widget config form\\n(item + yellow/red thresholds)\" as Form\nparticipant \"Server-side form validation\\n+ widget save handler\" as Save\ncollections \"Persisted widget config\" as Config\nparticipant \"Dashboard refresh lifecycle\\n/widget action\" as View\nparticipant \"Item access + permission\\n+ latest value retrieval\" as ItemFlow\nparticipant \"Threshold evaluator\\n(single source of truth)\" as Eval\nparticipant \"Traffic Light template/JS\\nrender state\" as Render\n\nUser -> Form : Select item\\nEnter thresholds\nForm -> Save : Submit config\nSave -> Save : Validate required item\\nValidate numeric inputs\\nValidate supported numeric type\\nValidate threshold ordering\nSave -> Config : Persist itemid + thresholds\n\nloop Open widget / dashboard refresh\n  View -> Config : Load widget config\n  View -> ItemFlow : Resolve item + check visibility\\nFetch latest value\n\n  alt Item missing or denied\n    ItemFlow --> View : INACCESSIBLE\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else Unsupported item type\n    ItemFlow --> View : UNSUPPORTED\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else No latest value\n    ItemFlow --> View : NO_DATA\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else Numeric latest value\n    ItemFlow --> View : value\n    View -> Eval : Evaluate(value, thresholds)\n    Eval --> View : GREEN or YELLOW or RED\n    View -> Render : Render exactly one active light\\n+ optional formatted item/value\n  end\nend\n\n@enduml\n```",

                  "data": {
                    "title": "Add threshold-based Traffic Light dashboard widget",
                    "description": "## Goal\nImplement a new first-class **Traffic Light** dashboard widget that lets a user select a single numeric item and configure thresholds that deterministically map the item’s latest value to exactly one visual state: **GREEN**, **YELLOW**, or **RED**.\n\nThe widget must fit the existing dashboard widget architecture and lifecycle, including add/edit/save/reopen/refresh flows, reuse current item access and permission rules, and render a clear fallback state for missing, unsupported, inaccessible, or no-data cases.\n\n**Threshold semantics dashboard/item access patterns.\n- Evaluate the latest numeric value into exactly one state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- Return and render deterministic fallback states/messages for:\n  - missing item\n  - inaccessible item / permission denied\n  - unsupported item type\n  - no latest value\n- Integrate with normal dashboard refresh/update behavior so the state updates when the latest item value changes.\n- Preserve configuration across dashboard add/save/reopen/edit flows.\n- Add localization strings for widget name, config fields, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- Add non-numeric types are rejected or rendered as unsupported according to existing widget conventions.\n- Threshold ordering and equality behavior are explicitly defined in code and covered by automated tests.\n- For a supported numeric item with a latest value, the widget evaluates the value into exactly one active state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- The rendered widget never shows multiple The threshold model is explicit and consistent: `< yellow = GREEN`, `>= yellow and < red = YELLOW`, `>= red = RED`. Fallback rendering is stable for missing/inaccessible items, unsupported item types, and no-data cases.\n\nAdded the shared evaluator in [CTrafficLightWidgetHelper.php](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/include/classes/helpers/CTrafficLightWidgetHelper.php), unit coverage in [CTrafficLightWidgetHelperTest.php](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/tests/unit/include/classes/helpers/CTrafficLightWidgetHelperTest.php), and Selenium coverage in [testDashboardTrafficLightWidget.php](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/ui/tests/selenium/dashboardWidgets/testDashboardTrafficLightWidget.php). I also updated the backlog notebook and step checklist in [e1d89ba1-3016-4d4e-af14-27df5c935e46.md](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/.ploqa/backlog/notebooks/e1d89ba1-3016-4d4e-af14-27df5c935e46.md) and [step.md](/workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b/.ploqa/backlog/runs/e1d89ba1-3016-4d4e-af14-27df5c935e46/2026-04-18T15-05-36-361822-00-00/implement/implement/step.md).\n\nVerification completed here: `php -l` on the new PHP files, manifest JSON validation, and a direct PHP smoke check of threshold parsing/evaluation. Repo-managed suites are still blocked in this worktree because `phpunit` is not installed, `vendor/bin/phpunit` is absent, and `.ploqa/commands/commands.json` / `tfh-*` helpers are not present. The step checklist reflects that blocker, so the workflow step is not marked complete.\n\n`checklist_update.additions`: implement widget module; add targeted tests; run local verification; run repo-managed suites when tooling is available.  \n`checklist_update.updates`: first three items marked `[x]`; repo-managed suite execution left `[ ]` due missing tooling.\n[launcher] Codex exec exit code: 0\n\n\nSTDERR:\nWarning: Permanently added '[host.docker.internal]:2226' (ED25519) to the list of known hosts.\n\n\n## 5. sync workspace back to host\n$ ssh -i /workspace/.qemu/id_ed25519 -p 2226 -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=5 -o ConnectionAttempts=1 dev@host.docker.internal -- 'cd /home/dev/workspaces/b-3e66cb39-9875b178c858 && tar -cf - --exclude dbdata --exclude database_storage --exclude redis_data --exclude node_modules --exclude .pnpm-store --exclude .expo --exclude .local --exclude .cache --exclude .npm --exclude dist --exclude build --exclude .ploqa/codex-state --exclude '\"'\"'*/.ploqa/codex-state'\"'\"' --exclude .ploqa/tmp/codex --exclude '\"'\"'*/.ploqa/tmp/codex'\"'\"' --exclude .local --exclude .cache --exclude .npm --exclude .git --exclude .ploqa --exclude .launcher --exclude .mypy_cache --exclude '\"'\"'*/.mypy_cache'\"'\"' --exclude __pycache__ --exclude '\"'\"'*/__pycache__'\"'\"' --exclude .pytest_cache --exclude '\"'\"'*/.pytest_cache'\"'\"' --exclude .ruff_cache --exclude '\"'\"'*/.ruff_cache'\"'\"' --exclude .tox --exclude '\"'\"'*/.tox'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'.codex_runner_*'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/appendonlydir'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/pgdata'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/postgres_data'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/mysql_data'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/mongo_data'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/storage'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/uploads'\"'\"' --exclude '\"'\"'*/tmp'\"'\"' .' '|' tar --overwrite -xf - -C /workspace/generated_apps/_backlog_worktrees/3e66cb39-406b-4df1-a665-66f70f93c367/auto-35-add-dashboard-traffic-light-widget-for-t-e1d89b\nRETURN CODE: 0\n\nSTDOUT:\n\n\nSTDERR:\nWarning: Permanently added '[host.docker.internal]:2226' (ED25519) to the list of known hosts.\n",
                "data": {
                  "data": {
                    "title": "Add threshold-based Traffic Light dashboard widget",
                    "description": "## Goal\nImplement a new first-class **Traffic Light** dashboard widget that lets a user select a single numeric item and configure thresholds that deterministically map the item’s latest value to exactly one visual state: **GREEN**, **YELLOW**, or **RED**.\n\nThe widget must fit the existing dashboard widget architecture and lifecycle, including add/edit/save/reopen/refresh flows, reuse current item access and permission rules, and render a clear fallback state for missing, unsupported, inaccessible, or no-data cases.\n\n**Threshold semantics must be explicit in implementation and tests.** This ticket requires choosing and documenting one supported threshold model and one boundary rule for equality (`= threshold`), then applying that behavior consistently in validation, evaluation, rendering, and automated tests.\n\n## Scope\nIn scope:\n- Add a new dashboard widget type under the existing widget framework.\n- Register the widget so it is available in the widget picker and behaves like other first-class widgets.\n- Provide configuration for:\n  - exactly one monitored item\n  - threshold inputs required by the chosen three-state model\n- Validate required fields, numeric threshold input, supported item type, and valid threshold ordering.\n- Retrieve the latest value for the selected item using existing dashboard/item access patterns.\n- Evaluate the latest numeric value into exactly one state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- Return and render deterministic fallback states/messages for:\n  - missing item\n  - inaccessible item / permission denied\n  - unsupported item type\n  - no latest value\n- Integrate with normal dashboard refresh/update behavior so the state updates when the latest item value changes.\n- Preserve configuration across dashboard add/save/reopen/edit flows.\n- Add localization strings for widget name, config fields, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- Add automated coverage for validation, threshold evaluation, persistence, rendering, refresh behavior, and permissions.\n\nOut of scope:\n- Multi-item aggregation or rollups\n- Non-numeric thresholding\n- Arbitrary color schemes or more than three states\n- Advanced alerting logic beyond visual state display\n- New item retrieval/polling infrastructure outside existing widget/data access conventions\n\nSystem boundaries / changed surfaces:\n- Dashboard widget registration/catalog\n- Widget config form and persistence payload\n- Server-side widget view/action for render data\n- Threshold evaluation logic (single source of truth)\n- Client-side widget rendering/template/CSS\n- Localization resources\n- Automated tests across backend and UI/widget layers\n\n## System Changes\n- Add a new widget package under the existing UI widget architecture, following existing conventions for manifest, JS widget class, server-side view action, template, and styling.\n- Register the **Traffic Light** widget in the dashboard widget catalog so users can add, configure, save, reopen, and render it through standard flows.\n- Define widget configuration fields for:\n  - one selected item\n  - threshold values required by the chosen semantics\n- Implement server-side validation for:\n  - required item selection\n  - numeric threshold input\n  - supported numeric item type\n  - valid threshold ordering\n- Implement a single threshold evaluation path that maps one numeric value to one state only, plus fallback states such as `NO_DATA`, `UNSUPPORTED`, or `INACCESSIBLE`.\n- Reuse the existing latest-value retrieval flow used by dashboard widgets instead of introducing a new history or polling path.\n- Ensure refresh requests re-evaluate the latest value and current state without requiring widget reconfiguration.\n- Apply existing authorization and item visibility rules to both selection and render-time retrieval.\n- Render exactly three indicator lights, with only one active indicator for valid states and a deterministic fallback presentation for non-renderable states.\n- Display item name and formatted latest value only if this aligns with existing widget UX conventions and can reuse current formatting helpers.\n- Add localized widget metadata, labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- A user can add a **Traffic Light** widget from the dashboard widget picker.\n- The widget configuration supports selecting exactly one item and entering threshold values required by the implemented threshold model.\n- Saving and reopening the widget preserves the selected item and threshold configuration.\n- The selected item must be validated as a supported numeric item type; unsupported non-numeric types are rejected or rendered as unsupported according to existing widget conventions.\n- Threshold ordering and equality behavior are explicitly defined in code and covered by automated tests.\n- For a supported numeric item with a latest value, the widget evaluates the value into exactly one active state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- The rendered widget never shows multiple active lights at once.\n- If the selected item is missing, inaccessible, unsupported, or has no latest value, the widget shows a deterministic fallback state/message instead of an incorrect color.\n- The widget updates through the normal dashboard refresh lifecycle when the selected item’s latest value changes.\n- Users cannot obtain item data through this widget unless they are authorized to read that item.\n- Localized labels/messages exist for the widget title, config fields, validation errors, and fallback states.\n- Automated tests covering validation, evaluation, persistence, rendering, refresh behavior, and permissions pass.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n### Backend\n- [ ] Create a new widget module directory under the existing `ui/widgets` structure following established widget conventions.\n- [ ] Add widget metadata/manifest including widget id, name, namespace, action mapping/URL, defaults, JS class reference, and any required framework metadata.\n- [ ] Implement the server-side widget view/action that loads persisted config, resolves the selected item, applies permission checks, fetches the latest value, evaluates thresholds, and returns render data.\n- [ ] Add or extend widget form handling so the selected item id and thresholds are persisted in the widget configuration payload.\n- [ ] Validate required item selection, numeric threshold values, supported item value types, and valid threshold ordering.\n- [ ] Define one explicit threshold model and equality behavior for boundary values, and implement it as the single source of truth used during render-time evaluation.\n- [ ] Return deterministic fallback states for missing item, inaccessible item, unsupported item type, and no latest value.\n- [ ] Reuse existing item formatting helpers if the render payload includes item label and/or formatted value.\n- [ ] Ensure refresh-time requests re-evaluate state from the latest value using the standard dashboard lifecycle.\n- [ ] Add localization keys for backend-generated labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n\n### Frontend\n- [ ] Add a client-side widget class compatible with the existing dashboard widget lifecycle and wired from the widget manifest.\n- [ ] Implement widget rendering with exactly three indicator lights and only one active light for valid states.\n- [ ] Implement fallback UI for non-renderable states such as `NO_DATA`, `UNSUPPORTED`, or `INACCESSIBLE`, aligned with existing widget error/cover patterns.\n- [ ] Add widget configuration UI using existing item selector controls and numeric threshold field components where possible.\n- [ ] Add client-side validation for obvious required-field, numeric-input, and threshold-order issues while keeping server-side validation authoritative.\n- [ ] Ensure add/edit/save/reopen flows load and display persisted configuration correctly.\n- [ ] Ensure refresh/update behavior does not leave stale state or duplicate active indicators.\n- [ ] Add styles/assets that fit current dashboard theming and accessibility conventions.\n- [ ] Add localization strings for widget title, field labels, helper text, and fallback/state messages.\n\n### Testing\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for validation rules covering missing item, missing thresholds, non-numeric input, invalid ordering, duplicate/equal threshold scenarios if unsupported, and supported versus unsupported item types.\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for threshold evaluation covering values below, at, between, and above thresholds, with explicit assertions for equality behavior at every threshold boundary.\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for fallback handling for missing item id, inaccessible item, unsupported item type, and no latest value.\n- [ ] Add integration tests for widget save/load persistence across add, save, reopen, and edit flows.\n- [ ] Add integration tests for data retrieval and dashboard refresh so the active state changes when the underlying latest value changes.\n- [ ] Add permission-focused tests to verify users without item read access cannot retrieve item data through the widget.\n- [ ] Add frontend/rendering tests to verify only one light is active for valid states and fallback UI is shown for non-renderable states.\n- [ ] Run relevant project test suites for the touched backend and UI/widget areas using repository-standard helpers.\n- [ ] Manual verification: add the widget to a real dashboard, configure a seeded numeric item, and confirm visual rendering of GREEN, YELLOW, and RED states.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify threshold boundary behavior by setting exact threshold values and confirming the active light matches the documented equality semantics.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify save/reopen/edit flows preserve configuration and recalculate state correctly after threshold changes.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify refresh behavior by changing the latest item value and confirming the widget updates through the normal dashboard refresh lifecycle.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify fallback behavior for unsupported/no-data/inaccessible scenarios and confirm the widget does not show an incorrect active color.\n\n## Verification\nAutomated / tooling-based verification:\n- Run targeted unit and integration tests for the new widget module, threshold evaluator, and widget persistence/render paths.\n- Run relevant UI/widget test suites and backend test suites using repository-standard helpers (for example, `tfh-test` and targeted test invocations where available).\n- If needed, build and start the local stack using repository-standard helpers (for example, `tfh-build`, `tfh-start`, or equivalent) to validate end-to-end widget behavior.\n- Inspect logs for widget action/render failures during refresh or permission checks.\n\nManual verification:\n- Add the widget from the dashboard widget picker and confirm it appears and saves successfully.\n- Configure a supported numeric item and verify each state with representative values below, at, between, and above thresholds.\n- Verify exact threshold-edge behavior using boundary values equal to configured thresholds.\n- Reopen the widget configuration after save and confirm the selected item and thresholds persist.\n- Verify the widget updates after dashboard refresh when the latest value changes.\n- Verify behavior with a user lacking read access to the selected item.\n- Verify fallback rendering for missing item, unsupported type, and no-data cases.\n\n## Documentation Impact\n- Add or update widget catalog/help documentation for the new **Traffic Light** widget.\n- Document the chosen threshold semantics clearly, including required ordering and how equality at boundaries is resolved.\n- Add/update localization resources and translation source entries for widget labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- If the repository includes widget architecture or developer notes, add a short note describing how this widget is registered and how it reuses the standard latest-value and dashboard refresh lifecycle.\n\n## Suggested Diagram\n```puml\n@startuml\ntitle Traffic Light widget data flow\n\nactor User\nparticipant \"Widget config form\\n(item + yellow/red thresholds)\" as Form\nparticipant \"Server-side form validation\\n+ widget save handler\" as Save\ncollections \"Persisted widget config\" as Config\nparticipant \"Dashboard refresh lifecycle\\n/widget action\" as View\nparticipant \"Item access + permission\\n+ latest value retrieval\" as ItemFlow\nparticipant \"Threshold evaluator\\n(single source of truth)\" as Eval\nparticipant \"Traffic Light template/JS\\nrender state\" as Render\n\nUser -> Form : Select item\\nEnter thresholds\nForm -> Save : Submit config\nSave -> Save : Validate required item\\nValidate numeric inputs\\nValidate supported numeric type\\nValidate threshold ordering\nSave -> Config : Persist itemid + thresholds\n\nloop Open widget / dashboard refresh\n  View -> Config : Load widget config\n  View -> ItemFlow : Resolve item + check visibility\\nFetch latest value\n\n  alt Item missing or denied\n    ItemFlow --> View : INACCESSIBLE\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else Unsupported item type\n    ItemFlow --> View : UNSUPPORTED\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else No latest value\n    ItemFlow --> View : NO_DATA\n    View -> Render : Render fallback message/state\n  else Numeric latest value\n    ItemFlow --> View : value\n    View -> Eval : Evaluate(value, thresholds)\n    Eval --> View : GREEN or YELLOW or RED\n    View -> Render : Render exactly one active light\\n+ optional formatted item/value\n  end\nend\n\n@enduml\n```",

                    "rationale": "This refined ticket removes ambiguity and makes the work implementation-ready by explicitly defining scope boundaries, affected system surfaces, and required lifecycle integration points across widget registration, config persistence, server-side evaluation, rendering, refresh, permissions, and localization. It also turns the original draft into an actionable backlog item with structured acceptance criteria, separated backend/frontend/testing checklists, and clear manual-versus-automated verification guidance.                   "data": {
                    "data": {
                      "title": "Add threshold-based Traffic Light dashboard widget",
                      "description": "## Goal\nImplement a new first-class **Traffic Light** dashboard widget that lets a user select a single numeric item and configure thresholds that deterministically map the item’s latest value to exactly one visual state: **GREEN**, **YELLOW**, or **RED**.\n\nThe widget must fit the existing dashboard widget architecture and lifecycle, including add/edit/save/reopen/refresh flows, reuse current item access and permission rules, and render a clear fallback state for missing, unsupported, inaccessible, or no-data cases.\n\n**Threshold semantics must be explicit in implementation and tests.** This ticket requires choosing and documenting one supported threshold model and one boundary rule for equality (`= threshold`), then applying that behavior consistently in validation, evaluation, rendering, and automated tests.\n\n## Scope\nIn scope:\n- Add a new dashboard widget type under the existing widget framework.\n- Register the widget so it is available in the widget picker and behaves like other first-class widgets.\n- Provide configuration for:\n  - exactly one monitored item\n  - threshold inputs required by the chosen three-state model\n- Validate required fields, numeric threshold input, supported item type, and valid threshold ordering.\n- Retrieve the latest value for the selected item using existing dashboard/item access patterns.\n- Evaluate the latest numeric value into exactly one state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- Return and render deterministic fallback states/messages for:\n  - missing item\n  - inaccessible item / permission denied\n  - unsupported item type\n  - no latest value\n- Integrate with normal dashboard refresh/update behavior so the state updates when the latest item value changes.\n- Preserve configuration across dashboard add/save/reopen/edit flows.\n- Add localization strings for widget name, config fields, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- Add automated coverage for validation, threshold evaluation, persistence, rendering, refresh behavior, and permissions.\n\nOut of scope:\n- Multi-item aggregation or rollups\n- Non-numeric thresholding\n- Arbitrary color schemes or more than three states\n- Advanced alerting logic beyond visual state display\n- New item retrieval/polling infrastructure outside existing widget/data access conventions\n\nSystem boundaries / changed surfaces:\n- Dashboard widget registration/catalog\n- Widget config form and persistence payload\n- Server-side widget view/action for render data\n- Threshold evaluation logic (single source of truth)\n- Client-side widget rendering/template/CSS\n- Localization resources\n- Automated tests across backend and UI/widget layers\n\n## System Changes\n- Add a new widget package under the existing UI widget architecture, following existing conventions for manifest, JS widget class, server-side view action, template, and styling.\n- Register the **Traffic Light** widget in the dashboard widget catalog so users can add, configure, save, reopen, and render it through standard flows.\n- Define widget configuration fields for:\n  - one selected item\n  - threshold values required by the chosen semantics\n- Implement server-side validation for:\n  - required item selection\n  - numeric threshold input\n  - supported numeric item type\n  - valid threshold ordering\n- Implement a single threshold evaluation path that maps one numeric value to one state only, plus fallback states such as `NO_DATA`, `UNSUPPORTED`, or `INACCESSIBLE`.\n- Reuse the existing latest-value retrieval flow used by dashboard widgets instead of introducing a new history or polling path.\n- Ensure refresh requests re-evaluate the latest value and current state without requiring widget reconfiguration.\n- Apply existing authorization and item visibility rules to both selection and render-time retrieval.\n- Render exactly three indicator lights, with only one active indicator for valid states and a deterministic fallback presentation for non-renderable states.\n- Display item name and formatted latest value only if this aligns with existing widget UX conventions and can reuse current formatting helpers.\n- Add localized widget metadata, labels, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- A user can add a **Traffic Light** widget from the dashboard widget picker.\n- The widget configuration supports selecting exactly one item and entering threshold values required by the implemented threshold model.\n- Saving and reopening the widget preserves the selected item and threshold configuration.\n- The selected item must be validated as a supported numeric item type; unsupported non-numeric types are rejected or rendered as unsupported according to existing widget conventions.\n- Threshold ordering and equality behavior are explicitly defined in code and covered by automated tests.\n- For a supported numeric item with a latest value, the widget evaluates the value into exactly one active state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- The rendered widget never shows multiple active lights at once.\n- If the selected item is missing, inaccessible, unsupported, or has no latest value, the widget shows a deterministic fallback state/message instead of an incorrect color.\n- The widget updates through the normal dashboard refresh lifecycle when the selected item’s latest value changes.\n- Users cannot obtain item data through this widget unless they are authorized to read that item.\n- Localized labels/messages exist for the widget title, config fields, validation errors, and fallback states.\n- Automated tests covering validation, evaluation, persistence, rendering, refresh behavior, and permissions pass.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n### Backend\n- [ ] Create a new widget module directory under the existing `ui/widgets` structure following established widget                       "codex_prompt": "## Goal\nImplement a new first-class **Traffic Light** dashboard widget that lets a user select a single numeric item and configure thresholds that deterministically map the item’s latest value to exactly one visual state: **GREEN**, **YELLOW**, or **RED**.\n\nThe widget must fit the existing dashboard widget architecture and lifecycle, including add/edit/save/reopen/refresh flows, reuse current item access and permission rules, and render a clear fallback state for missing, unsupported, inaccessible, or no-data cases.\n\n**Threshold semantics must be explicit in implementation and tests.** This ticket requires choosing and documenting one supported threshold model and one boundary rule for equality (`= threshold`), then applying that behavior consistently in validation, evaluation, rendering, and automated tests.\n\n## Scope\nIn scope:\n- Add a new dashboard widget type under the existing widget framework.\n- Register the widget so it is available in the widget picker and behaves like other first-class widgets.\n- Provide configuration for:\n  - exactly one monitored item\n  - threshold inputs required by the chosen three-state model\n- Validate required fields, numeric threshold input, supported item type, and valid threshold ordering.\n- Retrieve the latest value for the selected item using existing dashboard/item access patterns.\n- Evaluate the latest numeric value into exactly one state: `GREEN`, `YELLOW`, or `RED`.\n- Return and render deterministic fallback states/messages for:\n  - missing item\n  - inaccessible item / permission denied\n  - unsupported item type\n  - no latest value\n- Integrate with normal dashboard refresh/update behavior so the state updates when the latest item value changes.\n- Preserve configuration across dashboard add/save/reopen/edit flows.\n- Add localization strings for widget name, config fields, validation errors, and fallback messages.\n- Add automated coverage for validation, threshold evaluation, persistence, rendering, refresh behavior, and permissions.\n\nOut of scope:\n- Multi-item aggregation or rollups\n- Non-numeric thresholding\n- Arbitrary color schemes or more than three states\n- Advanced alerting logic beyond visual state display\n- New item retrieval/polling infrastructure outside existing widget/data access conventions\n\nSystem boundaries / changed surfaces:\n- Dashboard widget registration/catalog\n- Widget config form and persistence payload\n- Server-side widget view/action for render data\n- Threshold evaluation logic (single source of truth)\n- Client-side widget rendering/template/CSS\n- Localization resources\n- Automated tests across backend and UI/widget layers\n\n## System
